1	"""Segmentation-aware metrics (course-correction C4) — make over-segmentation visible.
2	
3	Temporal IoU (``metrics.score``) is nearly blind to *fragmentation*: a single rally
4	chopped into three predicted pieces, or two rallies merged across a dead-time gap, can
5	still post a moderate mean-IoU. The temporal-action-segmentation / -localization
6	literature grades those failure modes with **F1@k** (segmental F1 at several overlap
7	thresholds) and **mAP@tIoU** (ranked, confidence-aware average precision over a tIoU
8	sweep). Reporting these alongside temporal-IoU is what lets a reconciler's split/merge/
9	tighten win actually show up — and stops an IoU-chasing rule from shredding rally
10	structure invisibly. See ``docs/ANALYZERS_AND_RECONCILERS.md`` §13/C4.
11	
12	This is **additive**: new functions over the existing ``metrics`` matcher; nothing here
13	changes ``score``/``compare``/LOVO behaviour. Pure (stdlib only), deterministic, no I/O —
14	so it runs in the GPU-free / numpy-free lanes too.
15	
16	Conventions match ``metrics``: an ``Interval`` is a ``(start, end)`` tuple of seconds.
17	A ``ScoredInterval`` is ``(interval, confidence)`` — the confidence is whatever the scorer
18	emits per predicted rally (e.g. the classifier probability), used only to *rank* for AP.
19	
20	Note on the segmental *edit* score: the classic TAS edit/Levenshtein score operates on the
21	ordered sequence of segment *labels* and is degenerate for a single action class — rally/
22	background segments strictly alternate, so the edit distance collapses to (twice) the
23	segment-count difference, adding nothing over ``segment_count_ratio``. So we intentionally
24	omit it and instead make over-segmentation explicit with ``merge_split_report`` (a bipartite
25	overlap-graph breakdown into merge/split/matched/missed/spurious — see below), alongside
26	``f1_at_overlaps`` (F1@k) + ``mean_average_precision`` (mAP@tIoU). Those, not a single-class
27	edit score, are the over-segmentation-sensitive metrics for this binary rally setting.
28	"""
29	from __future__ import annotations
30	
31	from typing import Dict, List, Sequence, Tuple
32	
33	from backend.eval.metrics import Interval, interval_iou, score
34	
35	#: A predicted interval plus the confidence used to rank it for average precision.
36	ScoredInterval = Tuple[Interval, float]
37	
38	#: Default overlap thresholds for F1@k — the TAS convention F1@{10,25,50}.
39	DEFAULT_OVERLAPS: Tuple[float, ...] = (0.1, 0.25, 0.5)
40	#: Default tIoU sweep for mean average precision — the action-detection convention.
41	DEFAULT_TIOUS: Tuple[float, ...] = (0.3, 0.4, 0.5, 0.6, 0.7)
42	
43	
44	def f1_at_overlaps(preds: List[Interval], gts: List[Interval],
45	                   overlaps: Sequence[float] = DEFAULT_OVERLAPS) -> Dict[float, float]:
46	    """Segmental F1 at each overlap threshold (``{overlap: f1}``) — the TAS F1@k.
47	
48	    Reuses the canonical greedy one-to-one matcher, so a fragmented prediction (3 pieces
49	    for 1 rally) scores extra false positives and a merged prediction misses a rally —
50	    both drag F1 down where temporal-IoU alone would not flinch.
51	    """
52	    return {ov: score(preds, gts, iou_threshold=ov).f1 for ov in overlaps}
53	
54	
55	def average_precision(scored_preds: Sequence[ScoredInterval], gts: List[Interval],
56	                      iou_threshold: float = 0.5) -> float:
57	    """Average precision at a single tIoU (all-points / VOC-2010 interpolation).
58	
59	    Predictions are ranked by descending confidence; each is a true positive iff it
60	    claims a still-unmatched ground-truth rally at IoU >= ``iou_threshold`` (highest
61	    available IoU wins, one GT per prediction). Returns AP in ``[0, 1]``. ``0.0`` when
62	    there are no ground-truth rallies or no predictions reach the threshold.
63	    """
64	    n_gt = len(gts)
65	    if n_gt == 0 or not scored_preds:
66	        return 0.0
67	
68	    order = sorted(range(len(scored_preds)),
69	                   key=lambda i: (-scored_preds[i][1], i))   # conf desc, stable by index
70	    matched_gt = set()
71	    precisions: List[float] = []
72	    recalls: List[float] = []
73	    cum_tp = 0
74	    cum_fp = 0
75	    for i in order:
76	        interval, _conf = scored_preds[i]
77	        best_iou, best_gi = 0.0, -1
78	        for gi, g in enumerate(gts):
79	            if gi in matched_gt:
80	                continue
81	            iou = interval_iou(interval, g)
82	            if iou >= iou_threshold and iou > best_iou:
83	                best_iou, best_gi = iou, gi
84	        if best_gi >= 0:
85	            matched_gt.add(best_gi)
86	            cum_tp += 1
87	        else:
88	            cum_fp += 1
89	        precisions.append(cum_tp / (cum_tp + cum_fp))
90	        recalls.append(cum_tp / n_gt)
91	
92	    # Monotone precision envelope (take the max precision to the right), then integrate
93	    # over recall steps: AP = sum_k (recall_k - recall_{k-1}) * precision_env_k.
94	    for i in range(len(precisions) - 2, -1, -1):
95	        precisions[i] = max(precisions[i], precisions[i + 1])
96	    ap = 0.0
97	    prev_recall = 0.0
98	    for p, r in zip(precisions, recalls):
99	        if r > prev_recall:
100	            ap += p * (r - prev_recall)
101	            prev_recall = r
102	    return ap
103	
104	
105	def mean_average_precision(scored_preds: Sequence[ScoredInterval], gts: List[Interval],
106	                           iou_thresholds: Sequence[float] = DEFAULT_TIOUS) -> float:
107	    """Mean of ``average_precision`` over a tIoU sweep (the standard mAP@tIoU)."""
108	    tious = list(iou_thresholds)
109	    if not tious:
110	        return 0.0
111	    return sum(average_precision(scored_preds, gts, t) for t in tious) / len(tious)
112	
113	
114	def segment_count_ratio(preds: List[Interval], gts: List[Interval]) -> float:
115	    """Predicted-to-GT segment-count ratio — a crude fragmentation diagnostic.
116	
117	    ``> 1`` hints over-segmentation (more predicted pieces than rallies), ``< 1`` hints
118	    merging/misses. Only meaningful with ground truth present; returns ``0.0`` if ``gts``
119	    is empty (use F1@k / mAP for the graded picture).
120	    """
121	    if not gts:
122	        return 0.0
123	    return len(preds) / len(gts)
124	
125	
126	def _overlaps(a: Interval, b: Interval) -> bool:
127	    """True iff two intervals share any positive temporal extent."""
128	    return min(a[1], b[1]) - max(a[0], b[0]) > 0.0
129	
130	
131	def merge_split_report(preds: List[Interval], gts: List[Interval]) -> Dict[str, float]:
132	    """Explicit over-/under-segmentation breakdown via the bipartite overlap graph.
133	
134	    Where ``segment_count_ratio`` only compares *counts* and ``f1_at_overlaps`` blends merge
135	    and split into one F1, this names the two failure modes directly — the diagnostic that
136	    surfaced our local-segmenter over-MERGE (one giant window swallowing many rallies). Build
137	    the bipartite graph (edge = any positive temporal overlap between a pred and a gt), take its
138	    connected components, and classify each by ``(#preds, #gts)``:
139	
140	      - ``(1,1)`` matched   ``(1,0)`` spurious (pred, no gt)   ``(0,1)`` missed (gt, no pred)
141	      - ``(1,>1)`` **merge** — one predicted window covers ≥2 rallies (over-MERGE; our failure)
142	      - ``(>1,1)`` **split** — one rally cut across ≥2 predictions (over-segmentation)
143	      - ``(>1,>1)`` complex (tangled many-to-many)
144	
145	    Returns counts plus ``merge_rate`` (fraction of GT rallies swallowed inside a merge group)
146	    and ``split_rate`` (fraction of GT rallies cut by ≥2 predictions) — the two headline
147	    over-segmentation rates. Pure, deterministic, stdlib-only.
148	    """
149	    n, m = len(preds), len(gts)
150	    p_adj: List[List[int]] = [[] for _ in range(n)]
151	    g_adj: List[List[int]] = [[] for _ in range(m)]
152	    for i, p in enumerate(preds):
153	        for j, g in enumerate(gts):
154	            if _overlaps(p, g):
155	                p_adj[i].append(j)
156	                g_adj[j].append(i)
157	
158	    seen_p: set = set()
159	    seen_g: set = set()
160	    comps: List[Tuple[set, set]] = []
161	    for start in range(n):
162	        if start in seen_p:
163	            continue
164	        ps: set = set()
165	        gs: set = set()
166	        stack: List[Tuple[str, int]] = [("p", start)]
167	        while stack:
168	            side, k = stack.pop()
169	            if side == "p":
170	                if k in ps:
171	                    continue
172	                ps.add(k)
173	                seen_p.add(k)
174	                stack.extend(("g", j) for j in p_adj[k] if j not in gs)
175	            else:
176	                if k in gs:
177	                    continue
178	                gs.add(k)
179	                seen_g.add(k)
180	                stack.extend(("p", i) for i in g_adj[k] if i not in ps)
181	        comps.append((ps, gs))
182	    comps.extend((set(), {j}) for j in range(m) if j not in seen_g)  # gts with no overlapping pred
183	
184	    counts = {"matched": 0, "merges": 0, "splits": 0, "spurious": 0, "missed": 0, "complex": 0}
185	    gts_merged = gts_split = 0
186	    for ps, gs in comps:
187	        a, b = len(ps), len(gs)
188	        if (a, b) == (1, 1):
189	            counts["matched"] += 1
190	        elif (a, b) == (1, 0):
191	            counts["spurious"] += 1
192	        elif (a, b) == (0, 1):
193	            counts["missed"] += 1
194	        elif a == 1 and b > 1:
195	            counts["merges"] += 1
196	            gts_merged += b
197	        elif b == 1 and a > 1:
198	            counts["splits"] += 1
199	            gts_split += 1
200	        else:
201	            counts["complex"] += 1
202	    out: Dict[str, float] = {k: float(v) for k, v in counts.items()}
203	    out["merge_rate"] = (gts_merged / m) if m else 0.0
204	    out["split_rate"] = (gts_split / m) if m else 0.0
205	    return out
206	
207	
208	def segmentation_report(scored_preds: Sequence[ScoredInterval], gts: List[Interval],
209	                        overlaps: Sequence[float] = DEFAULT_OVERLAPS,
210	                        iou_thresholds: Sequence[float] = DEFAULT_TIOUS) -> Dict[str, object]:
211	    """One-call bundle: F1@k + mAP@tIoU + per-tIoU AP + count ratio + merge/split breakdown.
212	
213	    ``scored_preds`` carry confidences for AP; F1@k + the merge/split breakdown use the bare
214	    intervals. Returns a plain dict (JSON-friendly, string keys) so it can ride next to a
215	    ``compare`` result or land in the experiment leaderboard.
216	    """
217	    preds = [iv for iv, _conf in scored_preds]
218	    return {
219	        "f1_at_overlaps": {f"{ov:g}": v for ov, v in f1_at_overlaps(preds, gts, overlaps).items()},
220	        "ap_at_tiou": {f"{t:g}": average_precision(scored_preds, gts, t) for t in iou_thresholds},
221	        "mean_ap": mean_average_precision(scored_preds, gts, iou_thresholds),
222	        "segment_count_ratio": segment_count_ratio(preds, gts),
223	        "merge_split": merge_split_report(preds, gts),
224	        "num_pred": len(preds),
225	        "num_gt": len(gts),
226	    }
227

7	from backend.eval import tolerance_metrics as tm

10	def test_exact_match_is_perfect():
11	    gts = [(0.0, 5.0), (10.0, 16.0), (20.0, 27.0)]
12	    matches, p, r, f1 = tm.match_1to1(gts, gts, tau_start=2.0, tau_end=1.5)

15	    assert all(ds == 0.0 and de == 0.0 for _, _, ds, de in matches)

17

33	    # but it WOULD match at a looser τ_end

36	
37	def test_over_production_costs_precision():

69	def test_boundary_error_report_splits_start_end():
70	    # unconditional best-overlap: gt0 best-overlaps pred0 (Δstart +1, Δend +3);
71	    # gt1 best-overlaps pred1 (Δstart -1, Δend +2). |Δstart| {1,1}, |Δend| {3,2}.
72	    gts = [(0.0, 10.0), (20.0, 30.0)]
73	    preds = [(1.0, 13.0), (19.0, 32.0)]

75	    assert rep["dstart"]["abs_median"] == pytest.approx(1.0)
76	    assert rep["dend"]["abs_median"] == pytest.approx(2.5)

81	def test_boundary_error_unconditional_surfaces_loose_end():
82	    # a pred whose end is far beyond τ (won't match in 1:1) STILL contributes its loose end here
83	    # — this is the survivorship-bias fix: the end-deficit must be visible, not hidden as a miss.
84	    gts = [(0.0, 5.0)]

92	def test_over_seg_report_flags_merge_and_split():
93	    # merge: one pred covers two rallies
94	    merge = tm.over_seg_report([(0.0, 30.0)], [(2.0, 8.0), (15.0, 22.0)])
95	    assert merge["merge_count"] == 1 and merge["split_count"] == 0
96	    # split: two preds cut one rally

98	    assert split["split_count"] == 1 and split["merge_count"] == 0

101	def test_tolerance_report_block_shape():
102	    gts = [(0.0, 5.0), (10.0, 16.0)]
103	    preds = [(0.3, 5.3), (10.0, 18.0)]  # #1 matches; #2 end too loose at τ_end=1.5

106	    assert rep["recall"] == pytest.approx(0.5) and rep["precision"] == pytest.approx(0.5)

1	"""Tests for backend.eval.rally_seg_eval — the pure scoring/aggregation core (no golden files,
2	no trajectory I/O). The windowing + trajectory parsing it composes are tested in their own modules.
3	"""
4	import pytest
5	
6	from backend.eval.rally_seg_eval import aggregate, evaluate, score_intervals, windows_for
7	from backend.eval.windowing import WindowingPreset
8	
9	GTS = [(0.0, 10.0), (20.0, 30.0), (40.0, 50.0)]
10	# A permissive preset so a short synthetic trajectory still forms a window.
11	_PRESET = WindowingPreset(name="t", velocity_thresh=0.02, merge_gap=2.0, min_window_duration=0.5)
12	
13	
14	def _write_oscillating_traj(path, fps=30, secs=4, width=1280):
15	    """A fast shuttle oscillating smoothly across the net (x=width/2) via a triangle wave →
16	    continuous high velocity (active) + many net crossings → one gated-survivable window."""
17	    lo, hi, period = 200, width - 200, 20  # frames per full oscillation
18	    rows = ["Frame,Visibility,X,Y"]
19	    for i in range(int(fps * secs)):
20	        phase = (i % period) / period          # 0..1
21	        tri = 2 * phase if phase < 0.5 else 2 * (1 - phase)  # triangle 0→1→0
22	        rows.append(f"{i},1,{lo + (hi - lo) * tri:.1f},300")
23	    path.write_text("\n".join(rows))
24	    return str(path)
25	
26	
27	def test_score_intervals_perfect():
28	    row = score_intervals(GTS, GTS)
29	    assert row["f1"] == pytest.approx(1.0)
30	    assert row["f1@0.25"] == pytest.approx(1.0)
31	    assert row["merge_rate"] == 0.0 and row["split_rate"] == 0.0
32	    assert row["merges"] == 0 and row["num_pred"] == 3 and row["num_gt"] == 3
33	
34	
35	def test_score_intervals_surfaces_over_merge():
36	    # One mega-window swallowing two rallies: tIoU-F1 is hurt AND merge_rate makes it explicit.
37	    preds = [(0.0, 30.0), (40.0, 50.0)]
38	    row = score_intervals(preds, GTS)
39	    assert row["f1"] < 1.0
40	    assert row["merges"] == 1
41	    assert row["merge_rate"] == pytest.approx(2 / 3)
42	    assert row["segment_count_ratio"] == pytest.approx(2 / 3)
43	
44	
45	def test_aggregate_means_across_videos_with_ci():
46	    rows = [score_intervals(GTS, GTS),                       # perfect
47	            score_intervals([(0.0, 30.0), (40.0, 50.0)], GTS)]  # over-merged
48	    agg = aggregate(rows)
49	    assert agg["n"] == 2
50	    # mean F1 sits between the two videos' F1s, and the CI block is present.
51	    assert 0.0 < agg["f1"]["mean"] < 1.0
52	    assert "ci_low" in agg["f1"] and "ci_high" in agg["f1"]
53	    assert agg["merge_rate"]["mean"] == pytest.approx((0.0 + 2 / 3) / 2)
54	
55	
56	def test_aggregate_empty():
57	    agg = aggregate([])
58	    assert agg["n"] == 0 and agg["f1"] is None
59	
60	
61	# --- W2: net-crossings gate (Gate-A) on the windowing -------------------------------- #
62	def test_windows_for_netcross_gate(tmp_path):
63	    pytest.importorskip("cv2")  # rally_gate import path tolerance (no cv2 actually needed)
64	    video = {"trajectory": _write_oscillating_traj(tmp_path / "osc.csv"),
65	             "fps": 30.0, "frame_width": 1280.0}
66	    ungated = windows_for(video, _PRESET, min_crossings=0)
67	    assert len(ungated) >= 1                                   # a window forms
68	    # The oscillating shuttle crosses the net many times → survives a modest gate...
69	    assert len(windows_for(video, _PRESET, min_crossings=2)) >= 1
70	    # ...but an absurd crossing requirement that would gate EVERY window out instead falls
71	    # back to the ungated set (never-empty safeguard) — the gate prunes, never zeroes.
72	    assert windows_for(video, _PRESET, min_crossings=999) == ungated
73	
74	
75	def test_evaluate_threads_min_crossings(tmp_path):
76	    video = {"name": "osc", "trajectory": _write_oscillating_traj(tmp_path / "osc.csv"),
77	             "fps": 30.0, "frame_width": 1280.0, "gts": [(0.0, 4.0)]}
78	    res = evaluate([video], _PRESET, min_crossings=3)
79	    assert res["min_crossings"] == 3 and res["aggregate"]["n"] == 1

41	## Current track (live) — newest first
42	
43	### R3 (cap re-scope) + R4 (rally-END inactivity trim) — measured on n=11 golden under R2 *(2026-06-17)*
44	The first quality levers scored on the **R2 task-faithful metric** (tolerance-match `tolF1`, asymmetric
45	τ_start=2/τ_end=1.5; see [R2_EVAL_METRIC_DESIGN.md](R2_EVAL_METRIC_DESIGN.md)), on the **n=11** golden corpus
46	(now incl. 5 real ground-truth clips: mahadevpura_1/_2, GX010128, GX030094, Badminton_BXH_2).
47	
48	- **R3 — re-scope the cap (12 → 6 s).** The 12 s cap was too coarse. Sweep (W1, no R4): `tolF1` cap=6 **0.273**
49	  > cap=8 0.234 > cap=12 0.171. So R1's recommended cap is **6 s**, not 12. (Means-based lead across the
50	  sweep; confirm with the paired sign-test before locking the config value.)
51	- **R4 — rally-END inactivity trim** (`window_inactivity_end`, **default-OFF**). The measured gap is the
52	  rally-*end* (|Δend| ≫ |Δstart| on every ground-truth clip): after a rally the shuttle keeps moving *slowly*
53	  above `velocity_thresh` (picked up / walked), so the velocity window over-extends its END. R4 trims that
54	  post-rally drift tail back to the last frame whose trailing window stays dense with **fast** motion
55	  (velocity > `inactivity_rally_thresh`); only cuts (recall can't rise), START untouched (already ≈Gemini).
56	  `_trim_inactive_tail` in `tracknet_runner`, wired through WindowingPreset / IndexingConfig / served path /
57	  `rally_seg_eval --inactivity-end`.
58	
59	  **MEASURED (n=11, on top of W1 cap=6; sweet spot τ_end-window=1.5 s, rally_thresh=0.20, min_density=0.30):**
60	
61	  | metric | cap=6 | + R4 | Δ |
62	  |---|---|---|---|
63	  | tolF1 | 0.276 | **0.323** | **+0.047** (95% CI [+0.022,+0.072], **sign-test 8/0, p=0.008**) |
64	  | mean \|Δend\| | 5.71 s | **3.81 s** | −1.9 s (toward Gemini's ~1.0) |
65	  | mean split-count | 5.5 | 5.1 | net DOWN (only mahadevpura_2 +1; GX010128 4→2, BXH2 5→4) |
66	
67	  **R4 beats the hard-cap mechanism for ends:** hard-cap got |Δend| 1.07 s but split 19.5 (heavy
68	  over-production); R4 gets a *significant* tolF1 lift + tighter ends with splits **net-down** — it cuts at
69	  the real inactivity point, not a blind midpoint. Cumulative tolF1: baseline 0.096 → cap12 0.171 → cap6
70	  0.276 → +R4 **0.323**. Default-OFF; promote via config once R1's net-over-seg guard + corpus growth land.
71	
72	### REAL-FOOTAGE VALIDATION + first ground-truth at-par — over-merge fix confirmed; the gap is the rally-END *(2026-06-16)*
73	The capstone of the day: the W1/W2/W1.5 windowing work, measured on **6 real Kushagra/Yash clips** (fully-local
74	no-AI WASB, vs Gemini as a strong reference) and — for the first time — against **owner-hand-labeled ground truth**
75	on two of them. Full report: [`REAL_FOOTAGE_VALIDATION_REPORT.md`](REAL_FOOTAGE_VALIDATION_REPORT.md) (adversarially
76	verified — a 17-agent synthesis killed 6/12 of its own draft claims). Reusable at-par analyzer: `scratch/at_par.py`.
77	
78	**(1) The over-merge fix is real at scale** (full-6, local-vs-Gemini, Gemini=reference not truth):
79	
80	| aggregate (n=6 real clips) | baseline | W1 (cap=12) |
81	|---|---|---|
82	| windows (vs Gemini's 165) | 48 (mega-windows) | 250 |
83	| mean window length | 62.9s | **10.8s** |
84	| merge-groups (1 window ⊇ ≥2 rallies) | 27 | **10** |
85	| mean matched-IoU | 0.23 | 0.39 |
86	
87	The signature pathology (GX010129: **2 windows / 267s / 27 rallies trapped**, mIoU 0.0) is gone. **But W1 over-corrects**
88	into over-segmentation (250 vs 165, ~1.5×) and it's **clip-content-driven, NOT venue** (mahadevpura-0 over-splits 1.55×
89	like the GX clips — the "GoPro-vs-court" story is unsupported at n=3/venue).
90	
91	**(2) First ground-truth at-par** (vs human-labeled rallies; the honest scoreboard):
92	
93	| clip (golden rallies) | metric | Gemini (cloud) | best LOCAL (no-AI) |
94	|---|---|---|---|
95	| mahadevpura-2 (40) | F1@0.5 / F1@0.25 | 0.557 / 0.835 | 0.296 / 0.722 (W1+W2+hardcap) |
96	| GX010128 (52) | F1@0.5 / F1@0.25 | 0.813 / 0.857 | 0.457 / 0.756 (W1+W2) |
97	
98	Local is **~86–88% of Gemini on rally DETECTION (F1@0.25)** but only **~53–56% on tight boundaries (F1@0.5)**. On
99	GX010128 local even **out-recalls Gemini** — it overlaps all 52 golden rallies; Gemini matched only 39/52 (missed 13).
100	
101	**(3) 🎯 The gap is the rally-END, confirmed at n=2 — START is already at Gemini parity.** Boundary error (matched
102	pairs, median seconds):
103	
104	| clip | local \|Δstart\| | Gemini \|Δstart\| | local \|Δend\| | Gemini \|Δend\| |
105	|---|---|---|---|---|
106	| mahadevpura-2 | 1.74 | 1.46 | **3.12** | 1.19 |
107	| GX010128 | 0.78 | 0.98 | **2.12** | 0.76 |
108	
109	Local nails the START (≈ or better than Gemini); the **entire** residual gap is the rally-END over-extending (the
110	velocity-windowing ends at the last *in-flight* frame, not the last *sustained-active* one). → **next lever = a
111	sustained-inactivity rally-END rule (See et al.), NOT a generic boundary head and NOT a serve/START classifier
112	(START is solved).**

#!/usr/bin/env python
"""Run the FULL test suite for the badminton-highlight-indexer.

A single, discoverable entry point so you (or a reviewer / CI) can verify the
whole suite before opening a PR:

    python run_all_tests.py             # full suite, quiet
    python run_all_tests.py -v          # verbose (passthrough to pytest)
    python run_all_tests.py -k config   # filter by keyword (passthrough)
    python run_all_tests.py --cov=backend --cov-report=term-missing   # coverage

Anything after the script name is passed straight through to pytest.

Notes
-----
- The suite is fully mocked (no GPU, no network, no WSL) and runs offline.
- The observability **reporting** tests skip cleanly if the optional ``obsreport``
  package isn't installed (they use ``pytest.importorskip``). To exercise them too:
      pip install -e ../sports-obsreport --no-build-isolation
- Exit code is pytest's own, so this is CI / pre-PR friendly (0 = all green).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import importlib.util
import subprocess
import sys


def _installed(module: str) -> bool:
    try:
        return importlib.util.find_spec(module) is not None
    except (ImportError, ValueError):
        return False


def main() -> int:
    args = list(sys.argv[1:])
    # Default to quiet unless the caller chose a verbosity.
    if not any(a in ("-q", "-v", "-vv", "-vvv", "--quiet", "--verbose") for a in args):
        args = ["-q", *args]

### 132. user (2026-06-17T11:40:00.270Z)

=== tracknet_runner head (imports/logger) ===
"""TrackNet shuttle-trajectory runner (WSL2 subprocess adapter).

This bridges the Windows-side indexer to a TensorFlow TrackNetV4 install living
inside WSL2 (ADR-001 in docs/DECISIONS.md). The flow is:

    1. Translate the Windows video path to its WSL-visible form (and optionally
       stage it into the WSL filesystem to avoid slow /mnt/c per-frame reads).
    2. Invoke ``src/predict.py`` inside the TrackNetV4 conda env via ``wsl``.
    3. Read back the per-frame ``Frame,Visibility,X,Y`` CSV it writes.
    4. Convert that trajectory into high-motion "action windows" — the same
       candidate-rally shape HybridYoloSegmenter produces — so the rest of the
       pipeline (AI handoff, DB population) is unchanged.

Nothing here imports TensorFlow; all model code stays in WSL.
"""

import os
import csv
import shlex
import logging
import subprocess
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import List, Dict, Any, Optional, Tuple

from backend.pipeline.detectors.base import DetectorRunner, WslCommandMixin, wsl_tilde_quote

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


@dataclass

132	    # BOUNDED WINDOWING (over-merge fix W1, RALLY_QUALITY_RESEARCH.md §6). 0 = OFF (default →
133	    # behaviour unchanged). When > 0, a candidate window longer than this many seconds is
134	    # recursively split at its largest internal inactivity gap (≥ ``window_min_split_gap`` s —
135	    # the most likely rally boundary), so one window can't swallow several rallies + the dead
136	    # time between them. Never merges, only cuts (recall can't drop). Measured recommended value
137	    # 12.0 (golden n=6: ΔF1 +0.139, 6/0 sign-test, merge_rate 0.523→0.038); kept OFF until promoted.
138	    max_window_duration: float = 0.0

140	    # HARD-CAP fallback for W1: when True, an over-long window with NO internal inactivity gap
141	    # (a continuously-active trajectory — e.g. the real-footage mahadevpura-1 174s blob) is
142	    # chopped at its midpoint until ≤ max_window_duration, making the cap a true upper bound.
143	    # Only cuts (recall can't drop). OFF by default until measured/promoted.
144	    window_hard_cap: bool = False
145	    # R4 rally-END inactivity trim (s, 0 = OFF). After a rally the shuttle keeps moving slowly
146	    # (picked up / walked) above velocity_thresh, so the window over-extends its END (the measured
147	    # |Δend| gap vs golden). Trim the post-rally tail back to the last frame whose trailing
148	    # window stays dense with FAST motion (velocity > inactivity_rally_thresh). Only cuts. OFF by
149	    # default. Measured win at inactivity_end=1.5/rally_thresh=0.20/min_density=0.30 (n=11: ΔtolF1
150	    # +0.047, sign 8/0 p=0.008, |Δend| 5.7→3.8s) — see docs/RALLY_QUALITY_RESEARCH.md.
151	    window_inactivity_end: float = 0.0
152	    inactivity_rally_thresh: float = 0.08
153	    inactivity_min_density: float = 0.34

155	    store_yolo_candidates: bool = True
156	    ai_handoff_padding: float = 2.0
157	    ai_max_workers: int = 5
158	    # Width (px) the gemini segmenter re-encodes chunks to before upload. Stream-copied

255	    @staticmethod
256	    def trajectory_to_action_windows(
257	        points: List[TrajectoryPoint],
258	        fps: float,
259	        frame_width: float,
260	        chunk_start: float = 0.0,
261	        velocity_thresh: float = 0.02,
262	        merge_gap: float = 2.0,
263	        min_window_duration: float = 2.0,
264	        chunk_index: Optional[int] = None,
265	        max_window_duration: float = 0.0,
266	        min_split_gap: float = 0.5,
267	        window_hard_cap: bool = False,
268	        window_inactivity_end: float = 0.0,
269	        inactivity_min_density: float = 0.34,
270	        inactivity_rally_thresh: float = 0.08,
271	    ) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
272	        """Convert a shuttle trajectory into candidate rally windows.
273	
274	        A frame is "active" when the shuttle is visible AND its inter-frame
275	        displacement (normalised by frame width, per second) exceeds
276	        ``velocity_thresh`` — i.e. the shuttle is in flight. Active frames within
277	        ``merge_gap`` seconds of each other are grouped into one window; short
278	        windows are dropped. Mirrors HybridYoloSegmenter's windowing so the dict
279	        shape feeding the AI-handoff phase is identical.
280	
281	        ``max_window_duration`` (0 = OFF, the default → behaviour unchanged): a guard against
282	        the *over-merge* failure where "shuttle moving" is conflated with "rally in progress"
283	        and a single window swallows several rallies + the dead time between them. When set, any
284	        window longer than this is recursively SPLIT at its largest internal inactivity gap (the
285	        longest stretch with no in-flight shuttle — the most likely rally boundary), provided
286	        that gap is at least ``min_split_gap`` seconds. This is the bounded-windowing fix from
287	        ``docs/RALLY_QUALITY_RESEARCH.md`` §6 (W1); it never merges, only cuts, so recall cannot
288	        drop, and it is config-gated default-OFF until measured on the golden set.
289	
290	        ``window_hard_cap`` (default OFF) makes ``max_window_duration`` a TRUE cap: when an
291	        over-long window has NO internal inactivity gap to split on (a continuously-active
292	        trajectory — the shuttle never stops moving, e.g. the real-footage ``mahadevpura-1``
293	        blob where the gap-splitter alone left a single 174 s window), it is recursively
294	        hard-chopped at its temporal midpoint until every piece is ≤ the cap. Still only cuts
295	        (never merges), so recall cannot drop; gated default-OFF until measured.
296	        """
297	        if fps <= 0:
298	            fps = 30.0
299	        if frame_width <= 0:
300	            frame_width = 1280.0
301	
302	        # Compute per-frame normalised velocity from the last *visible* point.
303	        active = []  # (frame_idx, velocity)
304	        prev: Optional[TrajectoryPoint] = None
305	        for p in points:
306	            if not p.visible:
307	                prev = None  # break the trajectory; absent shuttle = not in flight
308	                continue
309	            if prev is not None:
310	                dframes = p.frame - prev.frame
311	                if dframes > 0:
312	                    dist = ((p.x - prev.x) ** 2 + (p.y - prev.y) ** 2) ** 0.5
313	                    velocity = (dist / frame_width) * (fps / dframes)
314	                    if velocity > velocity_thresh:
315	                        active.append((p.frame, velocity))
316	            prev = p
317	
318	        if not active:
319	            return []
320	
321	        max_gap_frames = int(merge_gap * fps)
322	
323	        def _finalize(group: List[Tuple[int, float]]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
324	            vels = [v for _, v in group]
325	            return {
326	                "start": group[0][0] / fps + chunk_start,
327	                "end": group[-1][0] / fps + chunk_start,
328	                "active_frame_count": len(group),
329	                "peak_velocity": max(vels),
330	                "mean_velocity": sum(vels) / len(vels),
331	                "track_id_count": 1,  # single shuttle; kept for window-shape parity
332	                "chunk_index": chunk_index,
333	            }
334	
335	        groups: List[List[Tuple[int, float]]] = []
336	        group = [active[0]]
337	        for af in active[1:]:
338	            if af[0] - group[-1][0] <= max_gap_frames:
339	                group.append(af)
340	            else:
341	                groups.append(group)
342	                group = [af]
343	        groups.append(group)
344	
345	        if max_window_duration > 0:
346	            groups = TrackNetRunner._split_overlong_groups(
347	                groups, int(max_window_duration * fps), int(min_split_gap * fps),
348	                hard_cap=window_hard_cap)
349	
350	        # R4 — rally-END inactivity trim (default OFF). Applied AFTER the cap split so each rally
351	        # piece has its own post-rally drift tail removed: the velocity windowing keeps a window
352	        # "active" while the shuttle drifts/gets-picked-up above threshold, over-extending the END
353	        # (the measured gap vs golden). Trim back to the last frame whose trailing window is still
354	        # dense with motion (rally on); a sustained low-density run = rally over. Only cuts.
355	        if window_inactivity_end > 0:
356	            tw = int(window_inactivity_end * fps)
357	            groups = [TrackNetRunner._trim_inactive_tail(g, tw, inactivity_min_density,
358	                                                         inactivity_rally_thresh) for g in groups]
359	
360	        windows = [_finalize(g) for g in groups]
361	        return [w for w in windows if (w["end"] - w["start"]) >= min_window_duration]
362

417	    def _split_overlong_groups(groups: List[List[Tuple[int, float]]], max_win_frames: int,
418	                               min_split_frames: int,
419	                               hard_cap: bool = False) -> List[List[Tuple[int, float]]]:
420	        """Recursively split any active-frame group longer than ``max_win_frames`` at its largest
421	        internal gap (≥ ``min_split_frames``) — an inactivity-aware cut at the most likely rally
422	        boundary. Splitting only subdivides existing groups (never merges/extends), so it cannot
423	        invent or drop coverage; a group with no gap ≥ the floor is left intact (a genuinely long
424	        rally) UNLESS ``hard_cap`` is set, in which case such a group is hard-chopped at its
425	        temporal midpoint until every piece is ≤ ``max_win_frames`` (makes the cap a true upper
426	        bound on continuously-active trajectories). Iterative worklist to bound stack depth."""
427	        if max_win_frames <= 0:
428	            return groups
429	        out: List[List[Tuple[int, float]]] = []
430	        work = list(groups)
431	        while work:
432	            g = work.pop()
433	            if len(g) < 2 or (g[-1][0] - g[0][0]) <= max_win_frames:
434	                out.append(g)
435	                continue
436	            best_i, best_gap = -1, -1
437	            for i in range(1, len(g)):
438	                gap = g[i][0] - g[i - 1][0]

443	                # hard_cap: chop at the temporal midpoint so the cap is actually enforced.
444	                if hard_cap:

### 166. assistant (2026-06-17T11:44:14.568Z)

"""R5 evidence — blob-fallback rescues the continuously-active blob (mahadevpura_1).

Compares the milestone (cap=6 + R4) vs milestone + R5 blob-fallback on the n=11 golden corpus,
under R2 tolF1. The R5 goal (REAL_FOOTAGE_VALIDATION_REPORT.md §5): remove the degenerate
0-match / 1-window outcome on the gap-less blob, and be a NO-OP on the already-bounded clips.
Pure offline (CPU)."""
from __future__ import annotations

import os
from dataclasses import replace

from backend.eval import eval_stats
from backend.eval import rally_seg_eval as rse
from backend.eval.windowing import SERVED, build_windows

OUT = "C:/Users/avidu/Projects/badminton-highlight-indexer/output"
MANIFEST = os.path.join(OUT, "human_lovo_manifest.json")

MILESTONE = replace(SERVED, max_window_duration=6.0, inactivity_end=1.5,
                    inactivity_rally_thresh=0.20, inactivity_min_density=0.30)
MILESTONE_R5 = replace(MILESTONE, blob_fallback=True)


def main():
    golden = rse.load_golden(MANIFEST, OUT)
    have = [g for g in golden if g["gts"] and os.path.exists(g["trajectory"])]
    m = {r["name"]: r for r in rse.evaluate(have, MILESTONE)["rows"]}
    r5 = {r["name"]: r for r in rse.evaluate(have, MILESTONE_R5)["rows"]}

    print("=== milestone (cap6+R4)  vs  + R5 blob-fallback (n=%d) ===" % len(have))
    print(f"  {'video':22s} {'mile tolF1':>10s} {'+R5 tolF1':>10s} {'mile #win':>9s} {'+R5 #win':>9s} {'changed?':>9s}")
    changed = []
    for n in sorted(m):
        a, b = m[n], r5[n]
        ch = "CHANGED" if (a["num_pred"] != b["num_pred"] or abs(a["tol_f1"] - b["tol_f1"]) > 1e-9) else "no-op"
        if ch == "CHANGED":
            changed.append(n)
        print(f"  {n:22s} {a['tol_f1']:>10.3f} {b['tol_f1']:>10.3f} "
              f"{a['num_pred']:>9d} {b['num_pred']:>9d} {ch:>9s}")

    am = rse.aggregate(list(m.values()))
    bm = rse.aggregate(list(r5.values()))
    print(f"\n  aggregate tolF1: {am['tol_f1']['mean']:.4f} -> {bm['tol_f1']['mean']:.4f}")
    names = sorted(m)
    d = eval_stats.paired_delta_ci([m[n]["tol_f1"] for n in names], [r5[n]["tol_f1"] for n in names])
    st = d["sign_test"]
    print(f"  paired Δ tolF1 {d['mean_delta']:+.4f} [{d['ci_low']:+.4f},{d['ci_high']:+.4f}] "
          f"sign(W/L/T)={int(st['wins'])}/{int(st['losses'])}/{int(st['ties'])} p={st['p_value']:.4f}")
    print(f"  videos changed by R5: {changed}  (R5 should ONLY touch the gap-less blob)")

    # --- Direct look at mahadevpura_1: the forced cuts + the degenerate-outcome fix ---
    blob = next(g for g in have if g["name"] == "mahadevpura_1")
    wins_mile = build_windows(rse.TrackNetRunner_parse(blob), blob["fps"], blob["frame_width"], MILESTONE) \
        if hasattr(rse, "TrackNetRunner_parse") else None
    from backend.pipeline.detectors.tracknet_runner import TrackNetRunner
    pts = TrackNetRunner.parse_trajectory_csv(blob["trajectory"])
    wins_mile = build_windows(pts, blob["fps"], blob["frame_width"], MILESTONE)
    wins_r5 = build_windows(pts, blob["fps"], blob["frame_width"], MILESTONE_R5)
    forced = [w for w in wins_r5 if w.get("forced_cut")]
    print(f"\n  mahadevpura_1 blob: milestone -> {len(wins_mile)} window(s) "
          f"(max dur {max((w['end']-w['start']) for w in wins_mile):.1f}s); "
          f"+R5 -> {len(wins_r5)} windows ({len(forced)} forced_cut), "
          f"max dur {max((w['end']-w['start']) for w in wins_r5):.1f}s (cap 6s)")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

### 169. assistant (2026-06-17T11:45:25.681Z)

Critical finding: R5 fired on **all 11 clips**, over-segmenting normal rallies (aggregate tolF1 0.323→0.317, *worse*) — because at cap=6 many real rallies are 6–15s with no internal gap, so "still over cap" catches far more than the blob. But on the actual blob it's the intended rescue: **mahadevpura_1 tolF1 0.000→0.238, 2 windows (max 65.8s)→32 (max 4.1s)**.

The §5 spec is specifically the *continuously-active blob* (174s, ~10× the cap) — not a 7s rally marginally over. R5 needs a **magnitude gate**: only force-chop groups that are still a large multiple of the cap. Let me add a `blob_factor` (default 3×) so R5 stays surgical.

114	# ---- W1: bounded windowing (max_window_duration split) -------------------------------- #
115	def _moving(a, b):   # visible, fast-moving shuttle over frames [a, b)
116	    return [TrajectoryPoint(frame=i, visible=True, x=100 + 60 * (i - a), y=300) for i in range(a, b)]
117	
118	
119	def _invisible(a, b):
120	    return [TrajectoryPoint(frame=i, visible=False, x=0, y=0) for i in range(a, b)]
121	
122	
123	# Two rallies merged across a sub-merge_gap dead time → ONE over-long window without the cap.
124	_MERGED_PTS = _moving(0, 121) + _invisible(121, 162) + _moving(162, 243)  # ~8s, 1.4s internal gap
125	
126	
127	def test_max_window_duration_off_keeps_over_merge():
128	    # Default (max_window_duration=0): the two rallies stay merged into one window.
129	    w = TrackNetRunner.trajectory_to_action_windows(
130	        _MERGED_PTS, fps=30.0, frame_width=1280.0, velocity_thresh=0.02,
131	        merge_gap=2.0, min_window_duration=0.3)
132	    assert len(w) == 1            # over-merge reproduced (the failure the cap fixes)
133	    assert (w[0]["end"] - w[0]["start"]) > 7.0
134	
135	
136	def test_max_window_duration_splits_at_inactivity_gap():
137	    # With a 4s cap the 8s merged window is split at its 1.4s internal dead-time gap → 2 windows.
138	    w = TrackNetRunner.trajectory_to_action_windows(
139	        _MERGED_PTS, fps=30.0, frame_width=1280.0, velocity_thresh=0.02,
140	        merge_gap=2.0, min_window_duration=0.3, max_window_duration=4.0, min_split_gap=0.5)
141	    assert len(w) == 2
142	    assert w[0]["end"] < w[1]["start"]
143	    assert all((x["end"] - x["start"]) <= 5.0 for x in w)
144	
145	
146	def test_max_window_duration_preserves_genuine_long_rally():
147	    # A single continuous 10s rally (no internal gap ≥ min_split_gap) is NOT split, even
148	    # though it exceeds the cap — bounded windowing only cuts at real dead time.
149	    w = TrackNetRunner.trajectory_to_action_windows(
150	        _moving(0, 301), fps=30.0, frame_width=1280.0, velocity_thresh=0.02,
151	        merge_gap=2.0, min_window_duration=0.3, max_window_duration=4.0, min_split_gap=0.5)
152	    assert len(w) == 1
153	    assert (w[0]["end"] - w[0]["start"]) > 9.0
154	
155	
156	def test_split_overlong_groups_pure():
157	    # Direct check of the splitter: a group with one big internal gap splits there.
158	    grp = [(i, 1.0) for i in range(0, 20)] + [(i, 1.0) for i in range(60, 80)]  # gap 20..60
159	    out = TrackNetRunner._split_overlong_groups([grp], max_win_frames=30, min_split_frames=10)
160	    assert len(out) == 2 and out[0][-1][0] == 19 and out[1][0][0] == 60
161	    # No qualifying gap (all consecutive) → unchanged even when over length.
162	    solid = [(i, 1.0) for i in range(0, 100)]
163	    assert TrackNetRunner._split_overlong_groups([solid], 30, 10) == [solid]
164	
165	
166	# ---- W1 hard-cap fallback: enforce the cap on continuously-active trajectories --------- #
167	def test_window_hard_cap_chops_continuous_rally():
168	    # The same continuous ~10s rally that bounded-windowing alone LEAVES intact (no internal
169	    # gap) is, with window_hard_cap=True, chopped at the midpoint until every piece ≤ the cap.
170	    w = TrackNetRunner.trajectory_to_action_windows(
171	        _moving(0, 301), fps=30.0, frame_width=1280.0, velocity_thresh=0.02,
172	        merge_gap=2.0, min_window_duration=0.3, max_window_duration=4.0, min_split_gap=0.5,
173	        window_hard_cap=True)
174	    assert len(w) >= 3                                          # the 10s blob is broken up
175	    assert all((x["end"] - x["start"]) <= 4.01 for x in w)     # every piece within the cap
176	    assert w[0]["start"] <= 0.1 and w[-1]["end"] >= 9.0        # coverage preserved (only cuts)
177	
178	
179	def test_split_overlong_groups_hard_cap_pure():
180	    solid = [(i, 1.0) for i in range(0, 100)]                   # 99-frame span, no internal gap
181	    # default: kept whole; hard_cap: chopped into pieces each spanning ≤ max_win_frames
182	    assert TrackNetRunner._split_overlong_groups([solid], 30, 10) == [solid]
183	    out = TrackNetRunner._split_overlong_groups([solid], 30, 10, hard_cap=True)
184	    assert len(out) >= 4
185	    assert all((g[-1][0] - g[0][0]) <= 30 for g in out)
186	    # only cuts, never drops/duplicates: the pieces partition the original frames exactly
187	    assert sorted(f for g in out for f, _ in g) == list(range(0, 100))
188	
189	
190	# ---- R4: rally-END inactivity trim (drop the post-rally slow-drift tail) --------------- #
191	def test_trim_inactive_tail_drops_slow_drift():
192	    # fast rally (vel 0.5) frames 0..60, then slow drift (vel 0.03, still > active 0.02) 61..200.
193	    group = [(i, 0.5) for i in range(0, 61)] + [(i, 0.03) for i in range(61, 201)]
194	    out = TrackNetRunner._trim_inactive_tail(group, trail_frames=30, min_density=0.3, rally_thresh=0.2)
195	    assert out[0][0] == 0                        # start untouched
196	    assert out[-1][0] < 100                       # the pure-drift tail (frames 100..200) is trimmed off
197	    assert len(group) - len(out) >= 100           # ~all the slow drift cut (kept only a short decay tail)
198	
199	
200	def test_trim_inactive_tail_keeps_continuous_rally_and_is_noop_when_off():
201	    allfast = [(i, 0.5) for i in range(0, 100)]
202	    assert TrackNetRunner._trim_inactive_tail(allfast, 30, 0.3, 0.2) == allfast   # fully-fast: untouched
203	    assert TrackNetRunner._trim_inactive_tail(allfast, 0, 0.3, 0.2) == allfast    # trail=0: OFF/no-op
204	
205	
206	def test_window_inactivity_end_trims_through_api():
207	    # one window of fast motion then slow drift (both visible & > velocity_thresh → one group).
208	    fast = [TrajectoryPoint(frame=i, visible=True, x=100 + 40 * (i % 5), y=300) for i in range(0, 90)]
209	    slow = [TrajectoryPoint(frame=i, visible=True, x=100 + (i % 2), y=300) for i in range(90, 300)]
210	    pts = fast + slow
211	    off = TrackNetRunner.trajectory_to_action_windows(
212	        pts, fps=30.0, frame_width=1280.0, velocity_thresh=0.02, merge_gap=2.0, min_window_duration=0.3)
213	    on = TrackNetRunner.trajectory_to_action_windows(
214	        pts, fps=30.0, frame_width=1280.0, velocity_thresh=0.02, merge_gap=2.0, min_window_duration=0.3,
215	        window_inactivity_end=1.0, inactivity_rally_thresh=0.2, inactivity_min_density=0.3)
216	    assert on and off
217	    assert on[0]["end"] < off[0]["end"]          # R4 trims the drift tail → earlier END
218	    assert on[0]["start"] == off[0]["start"]     # START unchanged

## R-series continued — R1 + R5 shipped, R1 product-go dispatched

**Three PRs, all off `origin/master` in isolated worktrees, shared-checkout protocol followed (branched from `origin/master`, re-verified HEAD before each commit, staged explicit paths):**

| PR | What | State |
|---|---|---|
| [#201](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/201) | **R1 net over-seg promotion guard** | ✅ merged (`8c68143`) |
| [#203](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/203) | **R5 blob-fallback** (default-OFF) | ✅ merged (`f5f5e52`) |
| [#204](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/204) | **R1 milestone config flip** | ⏳ **DRAFT — needs your product-go** |

I merged #201 and #203 myself per the verified-green-CI authority (both are default-additive / default-OFF, no shipped-behaviour change; full suite + ruff + mypy green locally and in CI). #204 changes shipped behaviour, so it's left as a draft.

### 1. R1 net over-seg guard (the blocker fix)
The strict "no split/merge increase on ANY video" rule mis-fires on the structural mega-window→bounded change. The honest finding from the n=11 data: raw merge **count** is non-monotonic (one window hiding 40 rallies = `count 1, rate 1.0`; bounding it into 2-glue pieces = `count 9, rate 0.5` — *fewer* rallies hidden). So the guard scores over-seg on **normalized rates** (`tolerance_metrics.over_seg_guard`, wired into `rally_seg_eval --vs`): passes iff corpus-mean weighted cost doesn't rise **and** no per-video `merge_rate` regresses. Strict verdict always reported alongside (revert = read `strict_passes`).

### 2. R1 evidence (n=11 golden, R2 `tolF1`)
- **baseline → milestone (cap6+R4): Δ +0.232 [+0.153,+0.304], sign 10/0/1, p=0.002**
- **cap12 → cap6: Δ +0.108, sign 10/0/1, p=0.002** — confirms cap=6 beats cap=12 via sign-test (R3 was means-only)
- cap6 → +R4: Δ +0.047, sign 8/0, p=0.008
- mean `merge_rate` 0.694 → 0.150 (drops on all 11) → **net guard PASS, strict BLOCK** (the artifact)

### 3. R5 blob-fallback
After the gap-split + R4 trim, force-chops any group still > **4× the cap** (the gap-less blob), logs it, flags `forced_cut`. The factor gate keeps it surgical (vs `hard_cap` which chops before R4 and over-segments normals). **mahadevpura_1: tolF1 0.000 → 0.238** (2 windows max 65.8s → 32 max 4.1s); do-no-harm elsewhere — a stratum tool, default-OFF.

---

## 📨 DISPATCH — your decision: R1 product-go

**The milestone (cap=6 + R4) clears the refined net-guard promotion bar** (Δ+0.232, 10/0, p=0.002; no honest over-seg regression). Draft [#204](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/204) is ready — it flips four `IndexingConfig` defaults. **One design question to settle before merging**, surfaced rather than decided unilaterally:

> The nightly-regression's `default` config reads `SERVED` *by design* ("never drift from served"). After the flip, `SERVED` **is** cap6+R4, so the nightly's `default == milestone` and its all-OFF drift baseline collapses. Pick: (a) drop the now-redundant `milestone` config and repurpose `default` to track the next proposed change, or (b) re-pin an explicit all-OFF control. Ping me and I'll finish the 3 test updates + nightly change in whichever direction you choose.

Also still open (can't do myself): the **IAA study** — a 2nd labeler re-labels 2–3 golden clips so R2's `τ_end` can be calibrated.

**Verified locally + in CI** for the merged PRs: full `run_all_tests.py` (841 passed / 7 skipped, coverage 80.65%), `ruff` + `mypy` clean. 1	# LOVO threshold re-sweep at n=13 — the milestone thresholds still hold
2	
3	> **STATUS: COMPLETED (2026-06-18).** Terminal-state snapshot. Re-scoring only (cached WASB
4	> trajectories, no GPU). Reproducible via `lovo_resweep.py` in this folder.
5	
6	## Trigger
7	The golden corpus grew from n≈6 (when cap=6 + R4 were tuned) to **n=13** — two Boxhill clips just
8	landed (GX010141 #12, GX010137 #13; see [`GOLDEN_VIDEOS.md`](../../../GOLDEN_VIDEOS.md)). Owner asked
9	the right question: *now that the corpus is bigger, is it worth re-calibrating ("retraining") the
10	windowing thresholds?* This is the honest, held-out answer.
11	
12	## Method — and why leave-ONE-out at this n
13	- **Unit held out = a whole VIDEO** (rallies within a clip are correlated — splitting them leaks).
14	- **Leave-one-video-out (LOOCV):** for each candidate cap, score every video; then for each held-out
15	  video, pick the cap that's best on the **other 12** and apply it to the held-out one. This is the
16	  only leakage-free way to ask "would re-fitting the cap help?" — the pick never sees the test video.
17	- **Why one, not k:** at small n you want maximum training data per fold and n re-scores is cheap, so
18	  LOOCV gives the least-biased estimate. Switch to **stratified k-fold** only at large n (hundreds),
19	  where holding out ~10–20% no longer starves training and running n folds is the cost bottleneck.
20	- **Metrics:** tIoU **F1@0.5** (corpus headline) + **R2 tolF1** (the metric R3/R4 were tuned on).
21	- **Swept:** the cap (R3 knob) ∈ {OFF, 4, 6, 8, 12} with R4 ON; plus R4 on-vs-off and milestone-vs-baseline (sign test).
22	
23	## Results (n=13)
24	
25	**tIoU F1@0.5** — global cap sweep (R4 ON):
26	
27	| cap | OFF | 4 | **6** | 8 | 12 |
28	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
29	| mean F1@0.5 | 0.255 | 0.468 | **0.474 ← best (current)** | 0.454 | 0.449 |
30	
31	- **LOVO cap-selection:** mean **0.474** — every one of the 13 folds picks cap=6 → identical to fixed cap=6 (Δ **+0.000**).
32	- **R4 on-vs-off @cap6:** 0.474 vs 0.436 — **sign 11-0-2, p=0.001** (R4 still helps).
33	- **Milestone (cap6+R4) vs baseline (off):** 0.474 vs 0.236 — **sign 12-0-1, p<0.001**.
34	
35	**R2 tolF1** — global cap sweep (R4 ON):
36	
37	| cap | OFF | **4** | 6 | 8 | 12 |
38	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
39	| mean tolF1 | 0.173 | **0.364 ← best** | 0.359 (current) | 0.330 | 0.281 |
40	
41	- **LOVO cap-selection:** mean **0.355** (12/13 folds pick cap=4, 1 picks 6) vs fixed cap=6 **0.359** → Δ **−0.004** (re-fitting is *worse*, within noise).
42	- **R4 on-vs-off @cap6:** 0.359 vs 0.320 — **sign 9-1-3, p=0.021** (R4 still helps).
43	- **Milestone vs baseline:** 0.359 vs 0.137 — **sign 12-0-1, p<0.001**.
44	
45	## Verdict
46	- **The tuned thresholds HOLD at n=13 — re-calibration gives no measurable benefit.** On tIoU, LOOCV
47	  picks cap=6 unanimously (13/13) and ties the fixed value exactly. On R2 tolF1, cap=4 edges cap=6 by
48	  **0.005** (noise), and crucially **LOOCV re-fitting to cap=4 does NOT generalize better** (Δ −0.004) —
49	  so there is no honest signal to move the cap off 6.
50	- **R4 still clears the bar** on both metrics (p=0.001 / p=0.021) — keep it.
51	- **The milestone still dominates baseline** (p<0.001 both metrics).
52	- **Confirms the "corpus is in *measure* territory, not *retrain* territory"** read: at this n the
53	  golden set's value is regression/held-out evaluation, not re-fitting a 1–2-parameter heuristic that
54	  was already validated. The "retrain" payoff arrives via the **learned fusion scorer** (a real model),
55	  not these thresholds — see `../2026-06-multivideo-lovo/` (learned model overtook the heuristic at n=3).
56	
57	## Pointers for future re-sweeps
58	- **Re-run `lovo_resweep.py` after each batch of new labels** (it's CPU-only, seconds; add the new clip
59	  via `scratch/ingest_golden.py` first). It self-reports the cap sweep + LOVO + R4 + significance.
60	- **The trigger to actually re-calibrate:** when LOOCV starts **beating** the fixed value by a margin
61	  that survives the sign test (Δ materially > 0, not the ±0.005 noise here). Until then, don't chase it.
62	- **One thing to watch:** the **cap=4-vs-6 wobble on R2 tolF1**. It's noise now; if cap=4 pulls ahead
63	  *consistently across folds* as n grows, switch the cap to 4. (tIoU is firmly cap=6.)
64	- **Heterogeneity / per-content calibration:** the corpus mixes singles/doubles/multicourt + 29.97/59.94 fps.
65	  A single global cap is fine now; revisit **per-content presets** only when the corpus is large enough to
66	  fit them held-out (deferred until ≫16, per `RALLY_QUALITY_RESEARCH.md`).
67	- **When n hits the hundreds:** switch LOOCV → **stratified k-fold** (balance folds across fps / sport /
68	  court-type) for cost; the unbiasedness argument for LOOCV stops paying for its n-fold expense.
69	- **Expand the sweep** beyond the cap: the R4 sub-params (inactivity_end / rally_thresh / min_density),
70	  velocity_threshold, and `window_min_split_gap` are all candidates — same LOOCV harness, more axes.
71	
72	## Links
73	- Live tracker: [`RALLY_DETECTION_GAP_CLOSURE.md`](../../../RALLY_DETECTION_GAP_CLOSURE.md) (the gap-closure project).
74	- Corpus registry: [`GOLDEN_VIDEOS.md`](../../../GOLDEN_VIDEOS.md). R-log: [`QUALITY_ITERATIONS.md`](../../../QUALITY_ITERATIONS.md) (R3 cap / R4 end-trim).
75	- Prior LOVO precedent (learned model vs heuristic): `../2026-06-multivideo-lovo/`.

## Yes — #204 still stands at n=13, and the headline is *stronger*

I re-ran the full ablation ladder on the current n=13 corpus (the 2 new Boxhill clips **GX010137** / **GX010141**) under the R2 `tolF1`, on current `origin/master` code:

| transition | n=11 (when I filed #204) | **n=13 (now)** |
|---|---|---|
| **baseline → milestone (cap6+R4)** | Δ+0.232, sign 10/0/1, p=0.002 | **Δ+0.222, sign 12/0/1, p=0.0005** |
| **cap12 → cap6** (R3) | Δ+0.108, sign 10/0/1, p=0.002 | **Δ+0.110, sign 12/0/1, p=0.0005** |
| cap6 → +R4 | Δ+0.047, sign 8/0/3, p=0.008 | Δ+0.040, sign **9/1/3**, p=0.0215 |
| net over-seg guard | PASS (merge_rate 0.694→0.150) | **PASS** (merge_rate 0.651→0.161, **0 rate regressions**) |

**The milestone win is stronger** (12/0 vs 10/0 on the headline; the only non-win is the mahadevpura_1 blob tie, same as before). **Both new clips improve** under the milestone (GX010137 0.448→0.685, GX010141 0.333→0.431). The net guard still **PASSES at every weight** with no per-video `merge_rate` regression.

**Independent corroboration:** a sibling session already ran a *different* harness at n=13 ([#214 `lovo_resweep.py`](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/214)) and its numbers match mine to 3 decimals (milestone vs baseline 12-0-1 p<0.001; R4 9-1-3 p=0.021), and its leave-one-video-out cap selection picks **cap=6 unanimously (13/13)** on tIoU.

**Two honest nuances** (neither breaks the flip):
1. **R4's marginal step softened** (8/0 → 9/1/3). The one loss is **GX010137** (−0.042) — R4's tail-trim slightly over-cuts an already-strong clip. Still significant (p=0.0215, CI excludes 0), and that clip's *overall* milestone delta is a large win.
2. **cap=4-vs-6 wobble on tolF1** (from #214): cap=4 edges cap=6 by 0.005 (noise) — but LOVO re-fitting to cap=4 generalizes *worse*, and tIoU is firmly cap=6, so **cap=6 stands**; worth re-checking as n grows.

10	+ ``docs/R2_EVAL_METRIC_DESIGN.md`` §4.5): the growing GOLDEN-finalized set is re-run every
11	night so a *late* change cannot silently lose an *earlier* win. It is **default-ADDITIVE** — it
12	does NOT change the promotion gate. It is a *tripwire*, not a re-training run: no GPU, no Gemini,
13	no WASB re-extraction (those are cached and unchanged night to night).
14	
15	Two tracked configs (both re-scored every night):
16	
17	* **DEFAULT** — the SERVED production windowing (all milestone knobs OFF), i.e. what users get
18	  today. Catches a regression to shipped behaviour.
19	* **MILESTONE** — the proposed R3+R4 flip (cap=6 s + rally-END inactivity trim), the candidate
20	  the rally-quality track is converging on (``docs/QUALITY_ITERATIONS.md`` R3/R4). Tracked so a
21	  code change can't quietly regress the milestone *before* it's flipped ON.
22	
23	Ground truth + trajectories are LOCAL (the manifest points at owned, minors-free trajectory CSVs
24	outside the repo + ``output/<name>_golden_features.json``); the corpus is NOT committed. So the
25	baseline is tied to the owner's local golden corpus and is regenerated with ``--update-baseline``
26	whenever the corpus grows or an intended improvement lands. (A cloud agent does not suffice — it
27	has the repo but not the cached trajectories; hence the local Windows Scheduled Task wiring in
28	``scripts/nightly_regression.ps1``.)

`inactivity_min_density 0.34→0.30`. **W2 net-crossings, W1.5 hard-cap, and R5 blob-fallback all stay OFF.**
This is the [Launch Report convention](../../) record made **before** the flip merged — total quality impact
on **both rulers** + the over-seg guard + honest caveats, for posterity.

> **Why this flip is safe to ship:** measured on the **n=13** golden corpus (the grown set, incl. the two
> Boxhill clips GX010137/GX010141), the milestone clears the **net over-seg promotion bar** with room to
> spare, and the verdict was **independently reproduced** (a different harness, #214) and **adversarially
> stress-tested** (5-lens LOO/seed/per-clip workflow). Nothing in the verification refuted it.

---

## 1. Total quality impact — BOTH rulers (n=13, leave-one-video-out unit = whole video)

The headline promotion metric is the **R2 task-faithful tolerance-match F1** (`tolF1`, asymmetric τ_start=2/
τ_end=1.5); **tIoU-F1@0.5** is kept as the secondary continuity ruler.

| config | **R2 tolF1** | **tIoU-F1@0.5** | mean \|Δend\| |
|---|---|---|---|
| baseline (all-OFF) | 0.137 | 0.236 | 44.6 s |
| + W1 cap=12 | 0.210 | 0.382 | 5.7 s |
| + cap=6 (R3) | 0.320 | 0.436 | 5.0 s |
| **+ R4 (milestone, shipped)** | **0.359** | **0.474** | **3.3 s** |

**Paired sign-tests (the promotion evidence):**

| transition | Δ tolF1 (95% CI) | sign (W/L/T) | p | Δ tIoU-F1 | sign | p |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **baseline → milestone** | **+0.222** [+0.152, +0.286] | **12/0/1** | **0.0005** | +0.238 [+0.159, +0.318] | 12/0/1 | 0.0005 |
| cap12 → cap6 (R3) | +0.110 [+0.070, +0.153] | 12/0/1 | 0.0005 | — | — | — |
| cap6 → +R4 | +0.040 [+0.015, +0.065] | 9/1/3 | 0.0215 | — | — | — |

The single non-win in baseline→milestone is **mahadevpura_1** (the continuously-active blob: tolF1 0.000→0.000,
a tie — it needs R5, which stays OFF). **Both new clips improve:** GX010137 0.448→0.685, GX010141 0.333→0.431.

## 2. Over-segmentation guard (the promotion gate)

The R1 **net** over-seg guard (`tolerance_metrics.over_seg_guard`) is the gate (not the strict per-video count
rule). Baseline → milestone:

- **NET verdict: PASS** — weighted (merge:split = 2:1) merge_rate/split_rate cost **1.303 → 0.429** (Δ −0.874).
- **mean merge_rate 0.651 → 0.161** — drops on **all 13** videos; **zero per-video merge_rate regressions**.
- **strict_passes = False BY DESIGN** (not a regression): the raw per-video *count* rule trips because splitting
  a mega-window lifts split_count from ~0 and the raw merge *count* is non-monotonic (one window hiding 40
  rallies = count 1/rate 1.0 → bounded into pieces gluing 2 neighbours = count 9/rate 0.5 — **fewer** rallies
  hidden). The guard scores the recall-meaningful *rate*; see [QUALITY_ITERATIONS.md](QUALITY_ITERATIONS.md) "R1".

## 3. Verification provenance (independent + adversarial)

- **Independent reproduction (#214 `lovo_resweep.py`, a different harness):** milestone vs baseline 0.137→0.359,
  sign **12-0-1, p<0.001**; R4 on-vs-off 0.320→0.359, sign **9-1-3, p=0.021** — matches to 3 decimals. Its LOVO
  cap-selection picks **cap=6 unanimously (13/13)** on tIoU.
- **5-lens adversarial workflow** (reproduce / leave-one-out / R4-loss audit / new-clip sanity / guard audit →
  synthesis): verdict **STANDS**. Every LOO fold (n=12) clears CI-excludes-0 AND p<0.05; worst fold (drop
  GX010128, the biggest gainer) still Δ+0.202, p=0.0010 → **no single clip drives the win**.

## 4. Honest caveats (recorded so they aren't relitigated)

1. **The W1 cap is the dominant lever; R4 is a marginal increment.** baseline→cap6 is ~+0.18 of the +0.222
   tolF1 gain; the R4 inactivity-end trim adds a *small but significant* +0.040 (p=0.0215, sign 9/1/3). Frame R4
   as "a small, significant end-precision gain" (mean |Δend| 4.96→3.31 s), not a co-equal driver.
2. **R4's one loss is GX010137** (tolF1 0.727→0.685, −0.042) — a tolerance-accounting artifact, **not** an R4
   failure: on that clip strict F1 *improves* (0.805→0.822), |Δend| *improves* (0.447→0.439), over-seg unchanged;
   R4 simply trimmed 4 trailing predicted segments (43→39, toward gt=34). The clip's *overall* milestone delta is
   a large win (+0.237).
3. **GX010141 is the worst-behaved clip post-milestone** (merge_rate still 0.448, ~30 s windows vs gt max ~10.5 s)
   — partially-merged, **not** a regression (it improves +0.098) and it does not break the guard, but it's a
   future over-merge target as the corpus grows.
4. **cap=4-vs-6 wobble on tolF1** (from #214): cap=4 edges cap=6 by ~0.005 (noise) — but **LOVO re-fitting to
   cap=4 generalizes *worse*** (Δ−0.004) and tIoU is firmly cap=6, so **cap=6 stands**. Watch as n grows; switch
   only if cap=4 pulls ahead *consistently across folds*.
5. **n=13 is small.** "Robust" here means "no single clip or bootstrap seed flips the verdict," not large-sample
   certainty. (Bootstrap CIs jitter ~0.001–0.004 run-to-run; means/sign-tests/p-values are exact.)
6. **Nightly self-stale (operational).** The nightly's `default` config IS SERVED, so post-flip it equals
   `milestone`; its config-drift detector will flag the frozen baseline **STALE** on the first post-flip run.
   **Action:** run `python scripts/nightly_regression.py --update-baseline` once after merge. (Optional follow-up:
   add a pinned all-OFF reference config to keep the pre-flip floor visible in the nightly.)
7. **Pure-logic eval only** (CPU, persisted trajectories) — no GPU/real-video re-detection was run; the flip
   changes only the windowing knobs the served path already reads.

## 5. What did NOT flip (still default-OFF, retained)

- **W2 net-crossings gate** — no ground-truth-backed lift (the weak-signals-retained directive keeps it).
- **W1.5 hard-cap** — over-segments normal clips (chops before R4); superseded by R5 for the blob case.
- **R5 blob-fallback** — a stratum tool for continuously-active clips; turn on selectively, not globally.

## What I did

**1. Verified #204 still stands at n=13** — and it holds *stronger* than at n=11:

| transition | n=11 (filed) | **n=13 (now)** |
|---|---|---|
| baseline → milestone tolF1 | Δ+0.232, 10/0, p=0.002 | **Δ+0.222, 12/0/1, p=0.0005** |
| cap6 beats cap12 | 10/0, p=0.002 | **12/0/1, p=0.0005** |
| net over-seg guard | PASS | **PASS** (merge_rate 0.651→0.161, 0 regressions) |

Both new Boxhill clips improve (GX010137 0.448→0.685, GX010141 0.333→0.431). Triple-confirmed: my re-measurement + the sibling's **#214 `lovo_resweep.py`** (different harness, matches to 3dp, LOVO picks cap=6 13/13) + a **5-lens adversarial workflow** that returned **STANDS** (every leave-one-out fold clears the bar; worst-fold drop still Δ+0.202, p=0.0010 — no single clip drives it).

**2. Made #204 actually mergeable and merged it** — rebased onto current master (resolved a config/R5 conflict), updated the 3 default-OFF assertion tests + nightly docstrings, and wrote the mandatory **[Launch Report](docs/R1_MILESTONE_LAUNCH_REPORT.md)** (both rulers + the over-seg guard + honest caveats). Full suite 855 green / cov 80.72%, ruff + mypy clean, 7/7 CI green → merged.

**3. Closed the one operational loose end** — **#216** re-baselined the nightly (post-flip its `default` config equals `milestone`, so it would have self-flagged stale).

## Honest caveats (recorded in the Launch Report)
- The **W1 cap is the dominant lever**; R4 is a small-but-significant +0.040 increment (1 loss = GX010137, a tolerance-accounting artifact — strict F1 and |Δend| both *improve* there).
- The gate is the **NET rate-based** over-seg guard; `strict_passes=False` is **by design** (the structural mega→bounded count artifact), not a regression.
- **GX010141** is the worst-behaved clip post-flip (merge_rate 0.448) — improves, not a regression, but a future over-merge target.
- One **optional follow-up** I deliberately left for you (it's the sibling's nightly design): add a pinned all-OFF reference config so the nightly keeps the pre-flip floor visible. The flip itself is complete without it.

A DDL ALTER auto-commits independently of the `with conn:` scope, so an interrupted upgrade (after ALTER, before the end-of-block `PRAGMA user_version` bump) leaves the column present + version stale → next open re-runs the ALTER → `OperationalError: duplicate column name` → **`__init__` throws forever = permanently bricked DB**. Fix: `_add_column_idempotent()` swallows ONLY 'duplicate column name'. Regression test proven to FAIL without the fix. Fully testable in CI (pure sqlite).
